ParlaMint-HR_2018-12-04-0.u109601	Thank you very much, Mr. President. At its 40th session, held this morning, the Labour, Pension System and Social Partnership Committee discussed the Final Proposals of the Law on Amendments and the Completion of the Law on the Supplement to Pensions by the Law on Pension Insurance on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Pension Insurance and the Law on the Statutory Insurance Statutory with an increased duration. The Committee discussed these acts in accordance with Article 83 of the Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament, as a home working body. In the opening statement concerning the Law on the add-on to pensions under the Pension Insurance Act, the Proposer pointed out that the proposed Law allows the exercise of the right to an allowance for pensioners who are compulsory in the compulsory retirement insurance of generational solidarity and compulsory pension insurance of individual capitalised savings, which will exercise the right to be compulsory from both pillars, which are defined in the first degree as a basic pension under the Pension Insurance Act, which, without fixing the allowance, would be in an unequal position with respect to users who exercise the right only in the first pillar. In view of the fact that a circle of users is extended to supplemental rights and to users who are compulsoryly insured in compulsory retirement insurance of individual capitalised savings, who, in compulsory retirement insurance of generational solidarity, make a pension defined in the first pillar as a basic pension under the Law, are required to make an allowance for those users in principle. For pensioners defined as basic pension under Article 91(2) of the Pension Insurance Act, which failed to achieve a pension period until the introduction of the second pillar, it is proposed to set an allowance as a percentage, 27% per basic pension with the application of the basic pension factor. In the debate, most members of the Committee supported the proposed solutions and after the debate, the Committee decided to propose to the Croatian Parliament to adopt the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Supplement to Pensions by the Pensions Act. As regards the second Law, the above-mentioned act was also discussed by the Committee in accordance with Article 83 of the Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament, as a home working body. In the opening statement, the minister pointed out the differences in the text of the final proposal in relation to the proposal, i.e. the promoter pointed out the differences in the text of the final proposal in relation to the text of the proposal. Thus, further harmonisation of the provision with the Final Proposal of the Law, which the Assembly had in its first reading, in order to strengthen the sustainability of the pension system, is proposed to speed up the age-to-age equality for women compared to men, and to increase the age by 4 months per year, starting from 01 January 2019, until the end of 2026. Since 01 January 2027, the conditions for the right to retirement for women and men to 65 years of life and 15 years of retirement would be equalised. The debate supports solutions that introduce the category of additional training to calculate the height of the pension to the parent, mother or adoptor of 6 months for each child born or adopted, that is to say, the parent’s father or adoptor if he used a large part of the maternity leave. ParlaMint-HR_2018-12-04-0.u110691	Dear Vice-President of the Assembly, honourable MPs and MPs, we discussed both the issue and the second pillar in the previous debate, but I will point out some newspapers in relation to the second reading. What I should certainly say was that the issue of abolishing the second pillar in any version was not a question. Thus, countries that have abolished the second pillar today look with envy at Croatia that has no potential. Poland, Hungary, Slovakia is working to reintroduce compulsory pension savings, as they realized there is no alternative for such pension savings. The story Mr. Lovrinović pointed out is one narrative fairy tale, I didn't recognize myself in it, it may be another Marko in the whole story, but what he would point out is that we went further developed a system, but just the same way we went towards introducing better management into the whole system, greater transparency and better risk control by the regulator. The second pillar did not answer, gave no answer for those who were in '62. every year and younger, especially 3,000 women who retire for the next two years, and that is why, in between, in the first reading and we introduced 27% supplements, or 3/4 of 27% for the second pillar, but we left both the right choice and the possibility for everyone to return to the first pillar on their retirement. What we have calculated is that this will certainly be more favourable for those who have a minimum wage of up to 70% 75% of the average salary and that those around 300,000 citizens who, with that addition within the combined pillar, cannot afford at least a minimum pension. The return of the 300,000 citizens to the first pillar will ensure the right to a minimum pension. In our first reading, we have already proposed a significant reduction in fees that do not go to anyone, I don't think they go to the state to go to the insurance company, in total when we look at the system of compensation from the draft until last year over 3 billion kunas was paid to pension funds. We still felt that there was room for savings but for the benefit of our insurance companies, citizens. By changing the Swiss formula for the second pillar, we have ensured that the wars of the second pillar will grow practically from the following year 7 to 9%, just because of the actuarial calculation, there is no need for these funds to be provided if the Swiss formula goes wild or if the pressure on wage growth is high. What's new with the first reading, so we went primarily to increase the competitiveness among funds, to start a competition match. And that's why there are two proposals, one to change the way REGOS deploys new insurance companies, but in my first reading, I pointed out that 98% of new users don't choose their second pension column. With the new proposal, 20% of all pension companies will get 4 of them, and 20% will be assigned to the highest bidder. The amendment of the HDZ Club of Representatives will further determine that the HANFA sets out a clear rule on what these criteria will be, which 20% of the insurance companies will be distributed. And so what we saw, that the significant reduction of first-reading fees was a billion kuna, that we would introduce an inequality between those smaller and larger compulsory pension societies, so that we limited or introduced a redistribution of those fees for those who have 20% and fewer shares and those who have 20% and more shares. In this way, the total reduction in fees by 2030 will be 1.32 billion kuna, but it will also be fairer in the sense that around 4 funds will have the same return on capital. In terms of better governance control and greater transparency together with HANF, we have made several proposals, which is that in the pension system or pension funds in the supervisory and audit committee, that the government appoints its representative that is not any appointment of commissioners as Mrsic said, these people will have to be professional, these people will have to have licenses, these people will have to follow all the rules and those people, which is most important, will have to be members of these companies. Therefore, these people will not be able to make decisions most will continue to be in the supervisory committees, but one representative of three or five depending on society will be a representative of the government, and we are interpreting this because both the Government and the Government have denied those 5% that once went to an office in the first pillar of solidarity for the benefit of society and the issue of, of course, better control and transparency. Also to avoid conflicts of interest, we have proposed that pension societies name independent members as their representatives in companies where they have shares or which they buy. There were a lot of questions to enable or not to invest in start-ups, there we also ran with a few ideas.
